# Phase 7.1 — Spine + AzerothCore family, Linux native Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** WotLK installs through the Install button on Linux with the typed Python engine — no bash — from a clean Ubuntu, Fedora 44 and Arch box, with the docker-group consent and the sudo password asked once, a mid-build cancel that resumes, and a compose config that matches the proven install.

**Architecture:** `catalog/native.py` becomes the game-free `StagedInstaller` spine (state file, guard, preflight, streaming) and `families/azerothcore.py` composes today's WotLK stages, names unchanged and pinned. `installer_for()` dispatches on `install.native`. The 7.1 catalog models (`EmulatorSource.dest`, `PasswordPlan`, `DbFacts`, `ReadyMarkers`, `NativeInstall.family/images/image_prefix/azerothcore`) make every per-game fact data. `docker.wait_ready(ReadySpec)`, SELinux facts + `{{BIND_LABEL}}`, `systemd-inhibit`, `SudoSession`, `install_wiring.py` and the compose-config fixture complete the Linux path.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.11+, pydantic (catalog models), PySide6 (untouched here), Docker CLI via `subprocess` (never the SDK), pytest + mypy + ruff + black; live gates on the Hyper-V VMs `yulon-ubuntu` (Docker Engine), Fedora 44 and Arch clean boxes, `yulon-win11` (Docker Desktop/WSL2).

**Spec:** `pyplan/phase7-decisions.md` (the design and the ten gated steps; this plan is step 7.1). Also binding: `pyplan/style-guide.md`, `pyplan/README.md`. Progress is ticked in `pyplan/checklist.md` under 7.1.

**Task order (keeps `pytest`/`mypy` green at every commit):** B.1, B.2, B.3, B.4, B.5, B.6, C.1, C.2, C.3, C.4, C.5, C.6, C.7, D.1, D.2, B.7, A.1, A.2, A.3, A.4, A.5, D.3, D.4, E.1, E.2, E.5, E.3, E.4

**Commit rules:** scoped commits per task; no "Generated with Claude Code" footer, no session link, no `Co-Authored-By: Claude` trailer (project rule). Never push to `main`; work is on `yulon-phase7`.

## Global Constraints

The interface contract below is copied verbatim from the design session. Every name, signature and type in it is final; its Amendments section (A1–A18) overrides the text above it. A task may add private helpers; it may not rename, re-type or re-home anything here.

<summary>Interface contract (binding)</summary>

# Phase 7 interface contract (binding for every plan task)

Every name, signature and type below is FINAL. A task may add private helpers; it may not
rename, re-type or re-home anything here. `pylauncher/` is the package root; paths are relative
to it. Python 3.11+, fully typed, `from __future__ import annotations`, pathlib, dataclasses,
pydantic for catalog models, `logging` via `yulon.log.get_logger(__name__)`, line length 100,
black + ruff + mypy clean. Tests: pytest, no daemon for unit tests, `tests/integration/` for
Docker-backed tests (self-skipping). Never a `print`. Never `Any` except at a `json.load()`
boundary. No `sudoers.d`, no `chmod` of the docker socket, no `usermod -aG docker` without the
existing consent path.

## 7.1 — spine, AzerothCore family, Linux native

### `yulon/catalog/native.py` (the spine)

```python
STATE_FILE = ".yulon-install.json"
STATE_VERSION = 1

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Secrets:
    db_password: str
    def __repr__(self) -> str: ...          # "Secrets(db_password=***)" — never the value

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class StageContext:
    server_dir: Path
    client_dir: Path | None
    state: InstallState
    cancel: threading.Event | None
    secrets: Secrets

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Stage:
    name: str
    run: Callable[[StageContext], Iterator[str]]
    recorded: bool = True
    cancel_note: str = ""

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class InstallState:
    game_id: str
    install_id: str
    family: str = ""                          # NEW; "" in files written before 7.1
    completed: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    last_error: str = ""
    updated_unix: int = 0
    version: int = STATE_VERSION
    def with_stage(self, stage: str, order: Sequence[str]) -> InstallState: ...
    def has(self, stage: str) -> bool: ...

def read_state(server_dir: Path, *, valid: Sequence[str]) -> InstallState | None: ...
def write_state(server_dir: Path, state: InstallState) -> None: ...

@dataclass
class Seams:                                 # every existing field keeps its name and type, plus:
    wait_ready: Callable[[docker.ContainerSpec, docker.ReadySpec], bool] = docker.wait_ready_for
    relabel: Callable[[Path], bool] = platform.relabel_for_containers
    selinux_enforcing: Callable[[], bool | None] = platform.selinux_enforcing
    fs_type: Callable[[Path], str | None] = platform.filesystem_type
    # ensure_docker is now CALLED WITH ask: self._seams.ensure_docker(cancel=cancel, ask=ask)

class StagedInstaller:
    """Abstract spine. Subclasses implement stages()."""
    def __init__(self, entry: CatalogEntry, *, installers_root: Path | None = None,
                 import_probe: docker.ImportProbe | None = None,
                 reset_unfinished: docker.ResetUnfinished | None = None,
                 seams: Seams | None = None) -> None: ...
    family: ClassVar[str]                     # "azerothcore" | "cmangos"; must equal entry.install.native.family
    def stages(self) -> tuple[Stage, ...]: ...            # abstract; names unique; never "preflight"/"guard"
    def stage_names(self) -> tuple[str, ...]: ...
    def server_dir(self, options: InstallOptions) -> Path: ...
    def preflight(self, options: InstallOptions, cancel: threading.Event | None = None, *,
                  ask: runner.Prompter | None = None) -> None: ...
    def run(self, options: InstallOptions | None = None, *, cancel: threading.Event | None = None,
            ask: runner.Prompter | None = None) -> Iterator[str]: ...
    # spine-provided stage bodies a family binds into Stage.run:
    def stage_clone_sources(self, ctx: StageContext, sources: Sequence[EmulatorSource]) -> Iterator[str]: ...
    def stage_generate_compose(self, ctx: StageContext) -> Iterator[str]: ...
    def stage_build(self, ctx: StageContext) -> Iterator[str]: ...
    def stage_start_db(self, ctx: StageContext) -> Iterator[str]: ...
    def stage_import(self, ctx: StageContext, gate: ImportGate, service: str | None) -> Iterator[str]: ...
    def stage_up(self, ctx: StageContext) -> Iterator[str]: ...
    def stage_ready(self, ctx: StageContext) -> Iterator[str]: ...
    # kept from NativeInstaller, unchanged in behaviour: _guard, _refuse_foreign_containers,
    # _preflight_lines, _pump, _check_run, _check_cancel, _held_awake, _record_error, _clone,
    # _remote_of, _replaceable_compose, _same_repo, _repo_key, _git_remote_url, _git_file_unmodified
    def resolve_secrets(self, server_dir: Path) -> Secrets: ...   # fixed → value; generated → read file or secrets.token_hex(8) with prefix

class ImportGate(Protocol):
    def probe(self) -> docker.ImportState: ...
    def reset(self) -> tuple[str, ...]: ...

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CallableGate:                          # ImportGate from two callables (the AC pair)
    probe_fn: docker.ImportProbe
    reset_fn: docker.ResetUnfinished | None
    def probe(self) -> docker.ImportState: ...
    def reset(self) -> tuple[str, ...]: ...  # raises InstallerError when reset_fn is None

# module constants kept: OPENING_NOTE, BUILD_CANCEL_NOTE, DOWNLOAD_CANCEL_NOTE, IMPORT_CANCEL_NOTE
# DELETED: NativeInstaller, STAGE_ORDER, NEVER_RECORDED, _READY_REALM_HOST, _run_stage and the
# nine _clone_core.._ready methods (their bodies move to families/azerothcore.py or become the
# stage_* methods above).
```

### `yulon/catalog/catalog.py` (7.1 subset)
```python
class EmulatorSource(Source):
    dest: str = Field(min_length=1)          # relative to the server dir; "." allowed
class Emulator(_Strict):
    sources: tuple[EmulatorSource, ...]
class PasswordPlan(_Strict):
    mode: Literal["fixed", "generated"]
    value: str | None = None                 # required when fixed
    file: str | None = None                  # required when generated, e.g. ".db_password"
    prefix: str = ""
class DbFacts(_Strict):
    image: str; client: Literal["mysql", "mariadb"]; user: str; charset: str = "utf8mb4"
class ReadyMarkers(_Strict):
    world: str; auth: str | None = None; fatal: str | None = None; timeout_s: int = 600; restart_loop: int = 4
class AzerothCoreData(_Strict):
    world_env: dict[str, str] = {}
class NativeInstall(_Strict):                 # existing fields kept (templates, soap_port, floors); world_env MOVES to azerothcore.world_env
    family: Literal["azerothcore", "cmangos"]
    images: tuple[str, ...] = Field(min_length=1)
    image_prefix: str = Field(min_length=1)
    dockerfile_dir: str | None = None
    db: DbFacts
    ready: ReadyMarkers
    azerothcore: AzerothCoreData | None = None
    # 7.3 adds: cmangos: CmangosData | None = None; validator: exactly the block `family` names is present
class Install(_Strict):
    password: PasswordPlan                   # REPLACES db_root_password / db_root_password_file (both deleted in 7.1)
    # script, script_platforms, script_variants, requires_client_dir, platforms, default_server_dir, native: unchanged in 7.1
```
catalog.json in 7.1: all four entries get `password` and source `dest`s; wow-wotlk gets the
`native` additions (`family`, `images`, `image_prefix`, `db`, `ready`, `azerothcore.world_env`)
and loses `script_platforms`; the other three keep `script` + `platforms: ["linux"]` and get NO
`native` block yet.

```python
def built_image_refs(entry: CatalogEntry, server_dir: Path, *, platform_id=platform.detect) -> tuple[str, ...]: ...
```

### `yulon/docker.py`
```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ReadySpec:
    world: str                                # regex, re.search over the world container's this-run log
    auth: str | None = None                   # regex over the auth log; None = do not wait on auth
    fatal: str | None = None                  # regex over the world log; a match → return False at once
    timeout: float = _READY_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
    interval: float = _POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS
    restart_loop: int = 4                     # RestartCount growth that means a crash loop → False
def wait_ready(auth_container: str, world_container: str, spec: ReadySpec) -> bool: ...   # SIGNATURE CHANGE
AZEROTHCORE_READY_WORLD = "ready..."          # the legacy wrapper's marker
def azerothcore_ready(realm_host: str, realm_port: int, **kwargs: float) -> ReadySpec: ...  # auth=re.escape(f"{host}:{port}"), world=AZEROTHCORE_READY_WORLD
def wait_ready_for(spec: ContainerSpec, ready: ReadySpec) -> bool: ...                     # SIGNATURE CHANGE
# controller.py:265 and controller_wow_wotlk/docker_ctl.py:53 call wait_ready_for(spec, azerothcore_ready(host, port, **kwargs))
# test_docker.py's four direct wait_ready callers pass ReadySpec.
```

### `yulon/platform.py`
```python
SELINUX_NOLABEL_FS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"exfat","ntfs","ntfs3","fuseblk","msdos","vfat","cifs","smb2","nfs","nfs4","9p"})
def selinux_enforcing(*, enforce_path: Path = Path("/sys/fs/selinux/enforce"), run: RunCmd | None = None,
                      which: Callable[[str], str | None] | None = None) -> bool | None: ...   # None = could not tell / not Linux
def filesystem_type(path: Path, *, run: RunCmd | None = None) -> str | None: ...           # `stat -f -c %T <path>`
def selinux_labels_supported(fs_type: str | None) -> bool: ...                               # None → True (deny-list)
def relabel_for_containers(path: Path, *, run: RunCmd | None = None,
                           which: Callable[[str], str | None] | None = None) -> bool: ...   # chcon -Rt container_file_t, no sudo
def bind_label(*, enforcing: bool | None, fs_type: str | None) -> str: ...                   # ":z" or ""
def container_user_args(*, platform_id: Callable[[], PlatformId] = detect) -> list[str]: ...  # ["--user", "uid:gid"] on linux, [] elsewhere; git.ContainerGit._user_args calls this
def keep_awake(...)  # Linux branch: spawn ["systemd-inhibit", "--what=idle:sleep", "--who=Yu'lon", "--why=installing a server", "sleep", "infinity"] detached; terminate on exit; OSError → warning + yield
def docker_engine_commands(pm, *, steamos) -> list[list[str]]   # dnf: ["dnf","-y","install","moby-engine","docker-compose","docker-buildx"]; pacman: [..., "docker","docker-compose","docker-buildx"]

RunWithInput = Callable[[list[str], str], subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]]
class SudoSession:
    """Asked once. `sudo -S -p ''` reads the password from stdin; every step gets it that way."""
    def __init__(self, ask: runner.Prompter, run_input: RunWithInput, *, attempts: int = 3) -> None: ...
    def argv(self, cmd: list[str]) -> list[str]: ...        # ["sudo", "-S", "-p", "", *cmd]
    def run(self, cmd: list[str]) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]: ...   # feeds password + "\n"
    def verify(self) -> bool: ...                            # `sudo -S -p '' -v`; asks up to `attempts` times; False when declined/wrong
    asked: int                                               # how many times ask() was called (test seam)
def _run_steps(do: RunCmd, commands: list[list[str]], *, sudo: bool, dry_run: bool,
               session: SudoSession | None = None) -> tuple[list[str], list[str]]: ...
    # a step whose `sudo -n` stderr contains "password is required" and session is not None → session.verify() once, then session.run(cmd) for it and every later step
def _ensure_docker_linux(..., ask=None, run_input: RunWithInput | None = None) -> ProvisionReport: ...  # builds SudoSession(ask, run_input) when ask is given
def ensure_docker(..., run_input: RunWithInput | None = None) -> ProvisionReport: ...
```

### `yulon/catalog/preflight.py`
```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Facts:            # existing fields plus:
    selinux_enforcing: bool | None = None
    server_fs_type: str | None = None
def gather(..., selinux: Callable[[], bool | None] = platform.selinux_enforcing,
           fs_type: Callable[[Path], str | None] = platform.filesystem_type) -> Facts: ...
# evaluate() adds Check("SELinux", "pass"|"warn"|"unchecked", ...): pass when not enforcing or labels supported;
# warn when enforcing and server_fs_type in SELINUX_NOLABEL_FS (":z" omitted; the daemon may refuse to
# create containers on this drive); unchecked when selinux_enforcing is None on linux.
```

## 7.2 — delete the bash lineage
- Delete: `catalog/installers/*/install-*.sh` (6), `wow-wotlk/dml-start.sh`, `wow-wotlk/wow-manage.sh`;
  `installer.Installer`, `PromptRule`, `PROMPT_RULES`, `ASK_THE_USER`, `make_responder`,
  `bash_available`, `NO_BASH_HELP`, `host_package_manager`, `platform_names`/`unsupported_platform_message`
  STAY (native uses them); `cancelled_install_message` STAYS (catalog_view uses it) but its script branch text is rewritten;
  `Install.script`, `script_platforms`, `script_variants`, `scripted_platforms()`, `uses_script()`, `script_for()`;
  `Install.is_native()` becomes `supports()` only; `Install.platforms` loses `min_length=1`;
  `tests/test_installer.py` script tests (keep dispatch/options tests), `tests/test_prompt.py` unchanged (the widget stays).
- Add: `catalog/installers/steam-deck/setup-gaming-mode.sh` (args: server dir, game id, ready regex).
- catalog.json: wow-tbc/vanilla/tortoise `platforms: []`, no `script`.
- `build/pylauncher.spec` datas: drop the scripts; keep `catalog/installers/**` templates + the steam-deck script.
- `pyplan/contribution.md` harness paragraph; style-guide §3 rows for `catalog/installer.py`, `catalog/catalog.py`.

## 7.3 — CMaNGOS family (data model, stage kinds, engine)

### `yulon/catalog/catalog.py` (7.3 additions)
```python
class Source(...)  # in yulon/manifest.py: rev: str | None = None  (commit pin; CloneSpec gains rev too; git.ContainerGit checks it out after clone)
MpqDepth = int | Literal["recursive"]
class ClientSpec(_Strict):
    required_file: str | None = None; min_mpq: int = 5; mpq_depth: MpqDepth = "recursive"
    locale_mpq_required: bool = False; near_client_warn_gb: float = 8.0
class DockerfileSpec(_Strict):
    make_jobs: int = 2
class ExtractTool(_Strict):
    name: str; argv: tuple[str, ...] = Field(min_length=1); produces: dict[str, int]   # dir under data/ → min files
class RetrySpec(_Strict):
    when_log_matches: str; tools: tuple[str, ...]                                  # tool names to re-run
class ExtractPlan(_Strict):
    image: str; tools: tuple[ExtractTool, ...] = Field(min_length=1); ulimit_stack_unlimited: bool = False
    retry: RetrySpec | None = None; stage_client: bool = False
class MmapPlan(_Strict):
    argv: tuple[str, ...]; min_files: int = 500; required: bool = True
class ConfPatch(_Strict):        # one file's patch table
    keys: dict[str, str]; match_commented: bool = False
class ConfPatchTable(_Strict):
    source_dir: str; files: dict[str, ConfPatch]
class SqlPhase(_Strict):
    name: str; into: str | None = None; into_each: dict[str, str] | None = None
    files: tuple[str, ...] = (); statements: tuple[str, ...] = (); gzip: bool = False
    sort: Literal["natural", "name"] = "natural"; on_error: Literal["fail", "warn"] = "fail"
class VerifyRule(_Strict):
    db: str; query: str; min: int
class PlayerData(_Strict):
    db: str; table: str; exclude_usernames: tuple[str, ...] = ()
class SqlPlan(_Strict):
    create: tuple[str, ...] = (); phases: tuple[SqlPhase, ...]; verify: tuple[VerifyRule, ...] = ()
    player_data: tuple[PlayerData, ...] = (); marker_db: str
    def plan_hash(self) -> str: ...          # sha256 of the canonical JSON, 16 hex
class CmangosData(_Strict):
    client: ClientSpec; dockerfile: DockerfileSpec; extract: ExtractPlan; mmaps: MmapPlan
    conf: ConfPatchTable; sql: SqlPlan
```

### `yulon/docker.py` (7.3 additions)
```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Mount:
    host: Path; guest: str; read_only: bool = False
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ContainerRun:
    image: str; argv: tuple[str, ...]; mounts: tuple[Mount, ...] = (); workdir: str | None = None
    env: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict); user_args: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    ulimits: tuple[str, ...] = ()            # e.g. ("stack=-1",) → --ulimit stack=-1
    def to_argv(self) -> list[str]: ...      # ["run", "--rm", ...] — pure, tested by field
def run_container(spec: ContainerRun, *, sink: OutputSink, cancel: threading.Event | None = None) -> AttachedRun: ...
def copy_from_image(image: str, src: str, dest: Path) -> None: ...     # create + cp + rm; raises DockerCommandError
def exec_stdin(container: str, argv: Sequence[str], source: BinaryIO, *, env: Mapping[str, str]) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]: ...
def sql_query(container: str, client: str, password: str, schema: str | None, statement: str) -> str: ...  # batch, skip-column-names, via exec; MYSQL_PWD env
```

### `yulon/catalog/families/` (7.3)
```python
# clientdir.py
def validate(client_dir: Path | None, spec: ClientSpec, *, free_bytes: Callable[[Path], int | None]) -> tuple[preflight.Check, ...]: ...
# dockerfile.py
def render(template_dir: Path, tokens: Mapping[str, str]) -> tuple[str, str]: ...        # (Dockerfile text, .dockerignore text), both starting with GENERATED_MARKER as a `#` comment
def write(server_dir: Path, dockerfile: str, dockerignore: str) -> tuple[Path, ...]: ...  # marker rule; refuses unmarked
# extract.py
EVIDENCE_FILE = ".yulon-extract.json"           # under data/
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ToolRecord: name: str; argv_hash: str; finished_unix: int
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Evidence: plan_hash: str; client_path: str; required_file_size: int | None; required_file_mtime: int | None; tools: tuple[ToolRecord, ...]
def read_evidence(data_dir: Path) -> Evidence | None: ...
def write_evidence(data_dir: Path, evidence: Evidence) -> None: ...
def tool_satisfied(tool: ExtractTool, data_dir: Path, evidence: Evidence | None, expected: Evidence) -> bool: ...
def run_plan(plan: ExtractPlan, *, image_ref: str, client_dir: Path, data_dir: Path, run_container: ..., user_args: Sequence[str], sink: OutputSink, cancel) -> Iterator[str]: ...
def run_mmaps(plan: MmapPlan, *, image_ref, data_dir, run_container, user_args, sink, cancel) -> Iterator[str]: ...
# conf.py
```

## Amendments (2026-08-26, after the first draft and the consistency check) — BINDING, override anything above

A1. **Dispatch in 7.1.** `installer_for()` returns `Installer` when `entry.install.native is None`, else `family_for(entry)(...)`. `Install.scripted_platforms()`/`uses_script()`/`is_native()` bodies are NOT changed in 7.1 (they are deleted in 7.2). wow-wotlk's Linux flip is therefore A.3's dispatch change; B.7 only edits catalog.json (drop `script_platforms`) and its tests. In 7.2 `installer_for()` raises `InstallerError` for `native is None`; `install_wiring.main()` catches `InstallerError` around `installer_for_app()` and exits 1.
A2. **`AzerothCoreData` holds only `world_env`.** `AzerothCoreInstaller._import` uses `entry.containers.db_import` (and `_client_data` uses `entry.containers.client_data`).
A3. **`native.INSTALL_REALM_HOST = "127.0.0.1"`** — public module constant in `native.py`; the spine fills `{{REALM_HOST}}` from it; `families/cmangos.py` imports it.
A4. **`Stage.cancel_note` is yielded by the spine** immediately after `--- <name>` when non-empty; stage bodies do not yield their own note (the exception text still carries it through `_check_run`).
A5. **`ReadyMarkers.regex: bool = False`.** When False the spine `re.escape`s the filled `world`/`auth`/`fatal` strings before building `ReadySpec`; Tortoise sets `"regex": true` for its alternations. `docker.azerothcore_ready()` keeps escaping.
A6. **`composegen.fill(text: str, tokens: Mapping[str, str]) -> str`** is PUBLIC (the old `_fill` becomes an alias kept for the existing tests); every module fills through it. **`composegen.entry_tokens(entry: CatalogEntry) -> dict[str, str]`** is PUBLIC (added by group G): the CMaNGOS set `DB_IMAGE DB_HOST DB_USER AUTH_DB WORLD_DB CHAR_DB LOGS_DB CONTAINER_PREFIX CORE_DIR CLIENT_BUILD MAKE_JOBS` (`LOGS_DB` only when `databases.extra` is non-empty — omit the key otherwise, never `""`). `CORE_DIR` = the in-image install prefix = `PurePosixPath(native.cmangos.conf.source_dir).parent` (`/opt/mangos`, `/opt/tortoise`). `CmangosInstaller._tokens(ctx)` = `entry_tokens(entry)` + `DB_PASSWORD` (from `ctx.secrets`) + `REALM_HOST` (`INSTALL_REALM_HOST`) + `PROJECT_NAME IMAGE_PREFIX IMAGE_TAG DB_PORT AUTH_PORT WORLD_PORT` — one mapping, built once.
A7. **`stage_import(ctx, gate, service)`** ALWAYS runs the five-branch probe table first (skip on `imported` / complete `populated`; refuse `unreadable` / incomplete `populated`; `partial` → `gate.reset()` then continue). When `service` is None it RETURNS after the branch table (the family applies the SQL itself and re-probes/writes the marker); when a service is given it runs the one-shot + `verify_import` as today. `stage_start_db` is unconditional (no `db_import` gate); the AC family may short-circuit in its own wrapper.
A8. **Secrets.** `resolve_secrets()` mints `f"{prefix}{secrets.token_hex(8)}"`; `.db_password` is written with a trailing newline and read with `.strip()`. Stage-tuple construction errors raise `ValueError` (a family bug, not a user error).
A9. **Preflight threads the client dir:** `StagedInstaller._preflight_lines()` calls `self._seams.gather(entry, server_dir, client_dir=options.client_dir, platform_id=..., docker_ready=...)` from 7.1 (A.3); `gather()` accepts `client_dir: Path | None = None` from 7.1 and ignores it until 7.3 adds `client_validate`.
A10. **`sqlplan.expand(plan, server_dir, schemas, tokens)`** fills every `statement` through `composegen.fill` (never a file). `schemas` is the identity mapping `{name: name for name in (db.auth, db.characters, db.world, *db.extra)}`, built by `CmangosInstaller._schemas()`. **`sqlplan.verify(plan, *, container, client, password, sql_query) -> tuple[str, ...]`** (failing rule names) is public; `_import` calls `verify()` then `write_marker()`; the family has no `_verify`. `apply()` yields warn lines with the path RELATIVE TO THE SERVER DIR (store `rel: str` on `PhaseRun`). Cancel notes: `extract.EXTRACT_CANCEL_NOTE`, `extract.MMAPS_CANCEL_NOTE`, `native.IMPORT_CANCEL_NOTE` — imported, never redefined.
A11. **`docker.exec_stdin(container, argv, source, *, env)`** passes each env key as a bare `-e KEY` and the VALUE through the subprocess environment (never `-e KEY=value` in argv). `sql_query()` runs as root. `ContainerRun.env` values DO land in argv (`-e K=V`) — never put a secret there.
A12. **`extract.run_plan(..., required_file: str | None = None, client_build: int | None = None)`** — defaulted keyword-only; `CmangosInstaller._extract` passes both. `run_mmaps` refuses when no extraction evidence exists.
A13. **`Source.rev`** is a full 40-hex SHA (`pattern=r"^[0-9a-f]{40}$"`). `CloneSpec.rev` too; all three `CloneSpec(...)` constructions (spine + both AC bodies) pass `rev=source.rev`.
A14. **`NativeInstall.dockerfile_dir`** is defined ONCE, in 7.1 (B.3). G.2 adds only `cmangos` and the exactly-one-family-block validator; B.6's `generated_entry()` gains a minimal `cmangos=` block in G.2.
A15. **7.2 contract corrections:** `tests/test_prompt.py`'s six `Installer`-bound tests go; the widget and `runner.interact()` transport tests stay. `bash_available()` moves verbatim to `tests/support_bash.py`. F.4 also updates `test_the_three_cmangos_entries_have_no_native_block_yet` (`platforms == ()`), deletes `test_wotlk_names_no_script_platform_but_still_ships_its_scripts_until_7_2`, and F.3 removes `--reinstall` from nothing — `install_wiring.main()` never had it (D.3 must not add it). `catalog_view.py`'s "exited 0 with no docker-compose.yml" copy is rewritten in F.3.
A16. **Byte snapshot.** B.6 commits `tests/data/wotlk-rendered/{docker-compose.yml,docker-compose.override.yml,docker-compose.build.yml}` rendered with `bind_label=""` for a fixed `server_dir` (`/home/pk/wow-server-playerbots`, platform `linux`) and a test asserting `render()` reproduces them byte for byte; G.5/G.7 keep that test green (the WotLK templates must not change in 7.3).
A17. **Docs per gate.** E.5 owns every style-guide §3 row for 7.1 (`install_wiring.py`, `families/__init__.py`, `families/azerothcore.py`, the `native.py` row wording) and the `pylauncher/README.md` capability-table/"Installing a server" edits that E.3/E.4's gate records trigger; A.3 adds NO style-guide rows. pyyaml goes in `requirements-dev.txt` (test-only). The yulon-ubuntu alias is `ssh yulon-ubuntu`; box mutations in E.3 (purging Docker, making sudo ask) are steps the owner approves first, said so in the task.
A18. **Naming that groups disagreed on:** Windows/dev citations of line numbers are re-derived by `git grep -n` at execution time (the tree moved by commit `72648ee4`); tasks say "grep for X" rather than a line number where the check flagged drift.

```python
"""App-side install wiring (phase7-decisions, "Wiring" row of the layout table).

D.3 fills this module in: `fixed_db_password()`, `import_gate_for()`,
`installer_for_app()` and the CLI harness `main()`. It exists this early
because `DEFAULT_DB_ROOT_PASSWORD` moves here the moment the catalog's
`db_root_password` field is deleted (the two were read together in `main.py`
and `controller_view.py`), and `controller_wow_wotlk.modules` re-exports it
from here so no importer has to move twice.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

DEFAULT_DB_ROOT_PASSWORD = "password"
"""acore-docker's default root password: what every fixed-password WotLK install shares with
backup, the console and every archived guide, and the fallback when an entry's plan has no
fixed value (moved from `controller_wow_wotlk.modules`, which re-exports it)."""
```

`yulon/catalog/catalog.py` — line 17 becomes `from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field, model_validator`; insert after `class Emulator` (after line 39):

```python
class PasswordPlan(_Strict):
    """How this game's database root password comes to exist (phase7-decisions "Password").

    `fixed` is WotLK's `"password"` — a contract with backup, the console and
    every archived guide, spliced into the base compose file as the
    `${DB_ROOT_PASSWORD:-…}` default. `generated` is the CMaNGOS entries':
    resolved by the install spine before stage 1 as `prefix + token_hex(8)`,
    persisted at `file` under the server dir, and reaching compose only
    through `.env`, never the compose text. One model rather than two optional
    strings because the old pair (`db_root_password`,
    `db_root_password_file`) let an entry say both or neither, and nothing
    refused either.
    """

    mode: Literal["fixed", "generated"]
    value: str | None = Field(default=None, description="The password itself; required when fixed.")
    file: str | None = Field(
        default=None,
        description=(
            "File under the server dir holding the generated value, e.g. `.db_password`; "
            "required when generated."
        ),
    )
    prefix: str = Field(
        default="",
        description=(
            "Prefix on a generated value (`tbc-`), so a password seen in a log or a `docker "
            "exec` says which server it belongs to. `resolve_secrets()` mints "
            "`f\"{prefix}{secrets.token_hex(8)}\"` — the dash is part of the prefix."
        ),
    )

    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def _the_mode_has_its_field(self) -> PasswordPlan:
        if self.mode == "fixed" and not self.value:
            raise ValueError("a fixed password plan needs a non-empty `value`")
        if self.mode == "generated" and not self.file:
            raise ValueError("a generated password plan needs `file`")
        return self
```

```python
        default=None, description="File under the server dir holding a generated password."
    )
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation** — catalog.py line 17 becomes `from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field, field_validator, model_validator`; change line 14 to `from pathlib import Path, PurePosixPath`; insert before `class Emulator` (line 35):

```python
class EmulatorSource(Source):
    """One repository the installer clones, and where it lands under the server dir.

    `dest` replaced an index rule ("sources[0] is the core, the rest go under
    `modules/`") that only AzerothCore's layout satisfied: CMaNGOS's playerbots
    checkout nests INSIDE the core at `src/mangos-tbc/src/modules/Bots`, which
    no index can say. Relative to the server dir, POSIX-spelled; `"."` means the
    server dir IS the checkout, as it is for AzerothCore.
    """

    dest: str = Field(
        min_length=1,
        description="Clone target relative to the server dir; '.' means the server dir itself.",
    )

    @field_validator("dest")
    @classmethod
    def _dest_stays_inside_the_server_dir(cls, value: str) -> str:
        path = PurePosixPath(value)
        if "\\" in value or path.is_absolute() or ".." in path.parts:
            raise ValueError(
                f"dest must be a relative POSIX path inside the server dir, got {value!r}"
            )
        return value
```

```python
def test_wotlk_native_block_names_its_family_images_database_and_ready_markers() -> None:
    """The facts `native.py` and `docker.py` hard-coded, now data (phase7-decisions "wow-wotlk").

    The image prefix and the four service keys are `composegen`'s old
    `DEFAULT_IMAGE_PREFIX`/`BUILT_SERVICES` with the same values; the ready
    markers are `docker.py`'s `"ready..."` literal and `native._READY_REALM_HOST`
    spelled as tokens. Same strings, new home, so a resume still finds its images.
    The markers are literal (`regex` False): the spine `re.escape`s them, so the
    dots in `127.0.0.1` never become wildcards.
    """
    native = load_catalog().get("wow-wotlk").install.native
    assert native is not None
    assert native.family == "azerothcore"
    assert native.images == ("worldserver", "authserver", "db-import", "client-data")
    assert native.image_prefix == "yulon.local/ac-wotlk-"
    assert native.dockerfile_dir is None
    assert native.db == DbFacts(image="mysql:8.4", client="mysql", user="root")
    assert native.db.charset == "utf8mb4"
    assert native.ready == ReadyMarkers(world="ready...", auth="{{REALM_HOST}}:{{WORLD_PORT}}")
    assert native.ready.fatal is None
    assert native.ready.regex is False
    assert native.ready.timeout_s == 600 and native.ready.restart_loop == 4
    assert native.azerothcore is not None
    assert native.azerothcore.world_env == {
        "AC_AI_PLAYERBOT_MIN_RANDOM_BOTS": "1600",
        "AC_AI_PLAYERBOT_MAX_RANDOM_BOTS": "2000",
    }
    # `world_env` moved INTO the azerothcore block; a stale top-level key is an error.
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError, match="world_env"):
        type(native).model_validate({**native.model_dump(), "world_env": {}})
```

```python
class DbFacts(_Strict):
    """The database the emulator runs on: image, client binary, app user, charset.

    Data because it differs per core (WotLK's `mysql:8.4` and `root`, the CMaNGOS
    entries' MariaDB and a `mangos` user) and because `apply.py`/`maintenance.py`
    spell the client binary today as a literal `mysql` — 7.9 reads it from here.
    """

    image: str = Field(min_length=1)
    client: Literal["mysql", "mariadb"]
    user: str = Field(min_length=1)
    charset: str = "utf8mb4"


class ReadyMarkers(_Strict):
    """What "the server is up" looks like in this game's logs, matched over this run's log.

    `world` is required: it is the line the `ready` stage waits for. `auth` is
    optional (None: do not wait on the auth log at all), `fatal` short-circuits
    the wait to failure. All three take the `{{TOKEN}}` grammar plus
    `REALM_HOST`, filled by the spine through `composegen.fill()`. They are
    LITERAL strings unless `regex` is true: the spine `re.escape`s the filled
    text before building `docker.ReadySpec`, so `127.0.0.1` matches only
    itself. Tortoise's alternations set `regex: true` (7.3). These are the
    literals `docker.py` ("ready...") and `native._READY_REALM_HOST` used to
    carry.
    """

    world: str = Field(min_length=1)
    auth: str | None = None
    fatal: str | None = None
    timeout_s: int = Field(default=600, gt=0)
    restart_loop: int = Field(
        default=4,
        ge=1,
        description="RestartCount growth that means a crash loop rather than a slow start.",
    )
    regex: bool = Field(
        default=False,
        description=(
            "True: `world`/`auth`/`fatal` are regular expressions as written. False: they are "
            "literal text the spine escapes before matching."
        ),
    )


class AzerothCoreData(_Strict):
    """The AzerothCore family's own install data — only the worldserver env block (A2)."""

    world_env: dict[str, str] = Field(
        default_factory=dict,
        description=(
            "Per-game runtime settings for the worldserver, merged over composegen's structural "
            "defaults. Data rather than Python because these are facts about ONE game that a "
            "person may reasonably want different: the playerbot population lives here, not in a "
            "module constant (style-guide §3, and an adversarial review that caught it there). "
            "PROVENANCE of WotLK's 1600/2000: copied from the ONE proven yulon-ubuntu install, "
            "where the Linux installer script wrote them, after a `docker compose config` diff "
            "on 2026-08-24 found a native install would otherwise differ from a script install. "
            "Not measured on any other machine and not measured at all for RAM — one desktop's "
            "population, carried so the two paths agree, owed an RSS reading by the first gate."
        ),
    )
```

```python
            "would clobber a pulled image and let a later `docker compose pull` silently "

            "loudly at pull time instead of booting a plausible-looking wrong server."
        ),
    )

        default=None,
        description=(

            "catalog/installers/ (CMaNGOS, 7.3). None: the checkout ships its own Dockerfile."
        ),
    )
    db: DbFacts
    ready: ReadyMarkers
    azerothcore: AzerothCoreData | None = Field(
        default=None, description="Present exactly when `family` is azerothcore (7.3 validates)."
    )
```

```python
def test_the_image_refs_match_the_services_the_build_overlay_actually_builds(
    tmp_path: Path,
) -> None:
    """`native.images` and the build overlay's `target:` lines must agree, and nothing else did.

    The same four names live in `build.yml.tmpl`'s `target:` lines, in
    `base.yml.tmpl`'s `image:` refs, and now in `catalog.json`'s `images`
    (they were a module constant, `BUILT_SERVICES`). Rename a `target:` without
    touching the data and `images_built()` asks the daemon for a reference that
    will never exist, so it answers False forever and every resume re-runs the
    multi-hour build — permanently, silently, with a green suite (review,
    2026-08-24). Derived from the rendered files rather than restated, so the
    assertion cannot drift the way the tuple did.
    """
    plan = render(tmp_path / "wow")
    refs = composegen.built_image_refs(ENTRY, tmp_path / "wow", platform_id=lambda: "linux")
    native = ENTRY.install.native
    assert native is not None

    built_targets = set(re.findall(r"^\s*target:\s*(\S+)\s*$", plan.build, re.MULTILINE))
    assert built_targets == set(native.images), (built_targets, native.images)
    assert all(ref.startswith(native.image_prefix) for ref in refs)
```

```python
def test_the_image_prefix_is_the_entrys_not_a_constant(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    """Two games must not tag their builds into one namespace; the prefix is catalog data."""
    native = ENTRY.install.native
    assert native is not None
    other = ENTRY.model_copy(
        update={
            "install": ENTRY.install.model_copy(
                update={"native": native.model_copy(update={"image_prefix": "yulon.local/x-"})}
            )
        }
    )
    refs = composegen.built_image_refs(other, tmp_path / "wow", platform_id=lambda: "linux")
    tag = composegen.image_tag(tmp_path / "wow", platform_id=lambda: "linux")
    assert refs == tuple(f"yulon.local/x-{name}:{tag}" for name in native.images)
    plan = composegen.render(
        other, tmp_path / "wow", templates_root=TEMPLATES, platform_id=lambda: "linux"
    )
    assert "yulon.local/x-worldserver:" in plan.base
    assert "yulon.local/ac-wotlk-" not in plan.base
    assert not hasattr(composegen, "DEFAULT_IMAGE_PREFIX")
    assert not hasattr(composegen, "BUILT_SERVICES")


def test_built_image_refs_refuses_an_entry_with_no_native_block(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    """Built from WotLK with `native` stripped, not from a CMaNGOS entry: G.4 gives those
    a native block in 7.3 and this test must not care."""
    scriptless = ENTRY.model_copy(update={"install": ENTRY.install.model_copy(update={"native": None})})
    assert scriptless.install.native is None
    with pytest.raises(composegen.ComposeGenError, match="install.native"):
        composegen.built_image_refs(scriptless, tmp_path / "wow", platform_id=lambda: "linux")
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation** — in `yulon/catalog/composegen.py` delete lines 75-85 (`DEFAULT_IMAGE_PREFIX` and its docstring) and lines 187-198 (`BUILT_SERVICES` and its docstring); replace the `built_image_refs` function (lines 201-220 at `665bfa58`) with:

```python
def built_image_refs(
    entry: CatalogEntry, server_dir: Path, *, platform_id: Callable[[], str] = platform.detect
) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    """The image references this install's build produces, fully qualified.

    Needed because "has this been built?" cannot be asked of compose. Measured
    on yulon-ubuntu (Docker 29.1.3, Compose 2.40.3, 2026-08-24): after a
    successful `compose -f… build`, `compose images -q` returns **nothing** —
    both bare and with the same `-f` set — and only starts answering once
    containers exist (`compose create` is enough; `up` is not required). Compose
    enumerates the images of a project's CREATED CONTAINERS, not of its service
    definitions, exactly as `images_built()`'s docstring feared.

    That window — built, no containers yet — is the whole window a resume asks
    in, so the old question answered "not built" for every finished build and a
    resume re-ran the compile. Cheap in BuildKit cache terms and still wrong:
    the engine would have reported hours of work it did not need to do.

    The prefix and the service keys are the entry's (`install.native.images`,
    `.image_prefix`) since 7.1, so two games cannot tag into one namespace.
    """
    native = _native_of(entry)
    tag = image_tag(server_dir, platform_id=platform_id)
    return tuple(f"{native.image_prefix}{name}:{tag}" for name in native.images)


def _native_of(entry: CatalogEntry) -> NativeInstall:
    """The entry's native block, or the one refusal every native-only function shares."""
    if entry.install.native is None:
        raise ComposeGenError(
            f"{entry.name} does not say how to install natively (no `install.native` in "
            "catalog.json), so no compose files were generated."
        )
    return entry.install.native
```

```python
HOST_BIND = re.compile(r"^\s*-\s*\./")


def test_every_host_bind_line_carries_the_label_and_no_named_volume_does(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    """SELinux: `:z` on every `./…:` bind, never on `client-data:`/`db-data:` (phase7-decisions).

    Eight lines today — seven in the base file, one in the override — and the
    count is asserted so a new bind line added without the token fails here
    rather than as "Permission denied" from a container on Fedora. A uniform
    `:z`, not the Fedora script's `:Z` on `./modules`: that mount is shared by
    `ac-db-import` and `ac-worldserver`, and `:Z` on a shared mount locks the
    other service out. The token may appear on nothing else — not even a
    comment, which `fill()` would otherwise happily rewrite.
    """
    base_tmpl = (TEMPLATES / "wow-wotlk/native/base.yml.tmpl").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    override_tmpl = (TEMPLATES / "wow-wotlk/native/override.yml.tmpl").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    assert base_tmpl.count("{{BIND_LABEL}}") == 7
    assert override_tmpl.count("{{BIND_LABEL}}") == 1
    for text in (base_tmpl, override_tmpl):
        for line in text.splitlines():
            if HOST_BIND.match(line):
                assert line.endswith("{{BIND_LABEL}}"), line
            elif "{{BIND_LABEL}}" in line:
                raise AssertionError(f"the label is on something that is not a host bind: {line}")

    labelled = composegen.render(
        ENTRY, tmp_path / "wow", templates_root=TEMPLATES, bind_label=":z", platform_id=lambda: "linux"
    )
    binds = [
        line for text in (labelled.base, labelled.override) for line in text.splitlines()
        if HOST_BIND.match(line)
    ]
    assert len(binds) == 8
    assert all(line.endswith(":z") for line in binds), binds
    assert "client-data:/azerothcore/env/dist/data/:ro" in labelled.base
    assert "db-data:/var/lib/mysql\n" in labelled.base
    assert labelled.build.count(":z") == 0


def test_off_selinux_the_label_renders_to_nothing(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    """The default is the empty string, so every existing install renders byte-identically."""
    plain = composegen.render(ENTRY, tmp_path / "wow", templates_root=TEMPLATES, platform_id=lambda: "linux")
    explicit = composegen.render(
        ENTRY, tmp_path / "wow", templates_root=TEMPLATES, bind_label="", platform_id=lambda: "linux"
    )
    assert plain == explicit
    assert ":z" not in plain.base and ":z" not in plain.override
    assert "- ./modules:/azerothcore/modules\n" in plain.override
    assert "{{" not in plain.base and "{{" not in plain.override


def test_a_bind_label_that_is_not_a_mount_option_is_refused(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    with pytest.raises(composegen.ComposeGenError, match="bind label"):
        composegen.render(
            ENTRY, tmp_path / "wow", templates_root=TEMPLATES, bind_label=":z\n  - /etc:/etc",
            platform_id=lambda: "linux",
        )


def test_fill_is_the_one_public_substitution_and_refuses_a_leftover_token() -> None:
    """A6: conf values, SQL statements and ready markers all go through this one function."""
    assert composegen.fill("a {{X}} b {{Y}}", {"X": "1", "Y": "2"}) == "a 1 b 2"
    assert composegen.fill("no tokens", {"X": "unused"}) == "no tokens"
    with pytest.raises(composegen.ComposeGenError, match="unfilled compose placeholder"):
        composegen.fill("a {{X}} {{Z}}", {"X": "1"})
    assert composegen._fill is composegen.fill
```

```python
def fill(text: str, tokens: Mapping[str, str]) -> str:
    """`{{TOKEN}}` substitution where an unresolved token is an ERROR.

    The ONE substitution for everything this engine renders — compose
    templates, a CMaNGOS Dockerfile, `.conf` values, SQL statements and the
    ready markers all come through here (A6), so a token spelled once in
    `catalog.json` means the same thing in every file. A template edit that
    adds a placeholder nobody fills would otherwise ship a compose file
    containing a literal `{{...}}`, which compose accepts happily as a string
    and which fails somewhere else entirely. Unused tokens are fine; unfilled
    ones are not.
    """
    out = text
    for key, value in tokens.items():
        out = out.replace("{{" + key + "}}", value)
    start = out.find("{{")
    if start >= 0:
        raise ComposeGenError(f"unfilled compose placeholder near: {out[start:start + 40]!r}")
    return out


_fill = fill
"""The pre-7.1 private name, kept so nothing that imported it moves twice."""
```

and change the three `_fill(` calls in `render()` to `fill(`.

```python
def generated_entry(tmp_path: Path, base: str) -> CatalogEntry:
    """wow-tbc (a generated-password entry) with a throwaway native block and templates.

    G.2's exactly-one-family-block validator makes this block need a minimal
    `cmangos=CmangosData(...)`; G.2 adds it here (A14).
    """
    templates = tmp_path / "gen"
    templates.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    (templates / "base.yml.tmpl").write_text(base, encoding="utf-8")
    (templates / "override.yml.tmpl").write_text("services:\n{{ENVIRONMENT}}\n", encoding="utf-8")
    (templates / "build.yml.tmpl").write_text("services:\n", encoding="utf-8")
    tbc = load_catalog().get("wow-tbc")
    native = NativeInstall(
        family="cmangos",
        templates="gen",
        images=("server",),
        image_prefix="yulon.local/cmangos-tbc-",
        db=DbFacts(image="mariadb:11", client="mariadb", user="mangos"),
        ready=ReadyMarkers(world="Avg Diff"),
    )
    return tbc.model_copy(update={"install": tbc.install.model_copy(update={"native": native})})

# -- A16: the committed byte snapshot ------------------------------------------

LINUX_SERVER_DIR = Path("/home/pk/wow-server-playerbots")
LINUX_INSTALL_ID = hashlib.sha256(b"/home/pk/wow-server-playerbots").hexdigest()[
    : composegen.INSTALL_ID_LENGTH
]
SNAPSHOT_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "data" / "wotlk-rendered"


def rendered_as_on_linux() -> dict[str, str]:
    """WotLK rendered for the yulon-ubuntu path, platform linux, no SELinux label.

    `install_id()` hashes `os.path.abspath()`, and on Windows `/home/pk/…` grows
    a drive letter, so the id is the ONE thing normalised to its Linux value —
    on Linux itself the replace is a no-op and the comparison is literal.
    """
    plan = composegen.render(
        ENTRY, LINUX_SERVER_DIR, templates_root=TEMPLATES, bind_label="", platform_id=lambda: "linux"
    )
    actual_id = composegen.install_id(LINUX_SERVER_DIR, platform_id=lambda: "linux")
    return {
        composegen.BASE_FILE: plan.base.replace(actual_id, LINUX_INSTALL_ID),
        composegen.OVERRIDE_FILE: plan.override.replace(actual_id, LINUX_INSTALL_ID),
        composegen.BUILD_FILE: plan.build.replace(actual_id, LINUX_INSTALL_ID),
    }


def test_the_wotlk_render_reproduces_the_committed_snapshot_byte_for_byte() -> None:
    """A16: the three files under `tests/data/wotlk-rendered/` ARE what `render()` writes.

    Committed in 7.1 and kept green through 7.3 (G.5/G.7), which is how "the
    WotLK templates render byte-identical" stops being a sentence in the
    checklist and becomes something the suite refuses to let drift. Regenerate
    them only by the command in B.6 Step 3, and only when a WotLK template
    change is the point of the commit.
    """
    texts = rendered_as_on_linux()
    assert set(texts) == {p.name for p in SNAPSHOT_DIR.iterdir() if p.is_file()}
    for name, text in texts.items():
        expected = (SNAPSHOT_DIR / name).read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        assert text == expected, name
    if sys.platform.startswith("linux"):
        assert composegen.install_id(LINUX_SERVER_DIR, platform_id=lambda: "linux") == LINUX_INSTALL_ID
    assert f"name: yulon-wow-wotlk-{LINUX_INSTALL_ID}\n" in texts[composegen.BASE_FILE]
    assert "${DB_ROOT_PASSWORD:-password}" in texts[composegen.BASE_FILE]
    assert ":z" not in texts[composegen.BASE_FILE]
```

### Task B.7: The Linux flip's data half — wow-wotlk loses script_platforms

Lands after C.1-C.7 and D.1/D.2 and immediately before A.1 (checker ordering). Per A1 this task edits ONLY `catalog.json` and tests: `Install.scripted_platforms()`/`uses_script()`/`is_native()` keep their bodies until 7.2 deletes them, and `installer_for()` is switched to `entry.install.native is None` by A.3. Consequence, stated so nobody is surprised: between this commit and A.3, `installer_for()` still dispatches on `is_native`, and an entry with no `script_platforms` reads as scripted everywhere — WotLK therefore gets the script `Installer` on all three platforms for that window. A.3 closes the window in the same gate (nothing ships in between); the tests below pin the transitional truth and A.3 rewrites them.

**Files:**
- Modify: `yulon/catalog/catalog.json` (wow-wotlk `install.script_platforms` deleted, lines 29-31 at `665bfa58`)
- Test: `tests/test_native.py` (the two dispatch tests — grep for `def test_the_platform_decides_which_engine_installs_and_linux_keeps_the_script` and `def test_script_platforms_defaults_to_platforms_so_old_entries_mean_what_they_said`; A.3 re-homes this file and rewrites both), `tests/test_catalog.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Install.native`, `Install.platforms`, `Install.script_platforms` (repo today, bodies unchanged)
- Produces: `wow-wotlk.install.script_platforms is None` in the shipped catalog — the data half of the 7.1 flip. A.3's `installer_for()` (`Installer` when `entry.install.native is None`, else `family_for(entry)(...)`) then dispatches WotLK natively on every platform; F.4 deletes `script_platforms` entirely and the `…until_7_2` test below (A15).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** — in `tests/test_native.py` replace the two dispatch tests named above with:

```python
def test_dispatch_until_a3_wotlk_without_script_platforms_reads_as_scripted_everywhere() -> None:
    """TRANSITIONAL — A.3 rewrites this the moment `installer_for()` dispatches on `native`.

    The data half of the 7.1 flip (B.7) drops `script_platforms`; the method
    bodies are untouched until 7.2 (A1), so `scripted_platforms()` falls back
    to `platforms` and `is_native()` is False everywhere. That is what the
    dispatcher sees until A.3 replaces `is_native(here)` with
    `native is None`. Pinned here so the window is a fact in the suite, not a
    surprise on a Mac.
    """
    assert ENTRY.install.script_platforms is None
    assert ENTRY.install.native is not None
    assert ENTRY.install.scripted_platforms() == ENTRY.install.platforms
    assert ENTRY.install.uses_script("linux") is True
    assert ENTRY.install.uses_script("macos") is True
    assert ENTRY.install.uses_script("windows") is True
    assert ENTRY.install.is_native("linux") is False
    assert ENTRY.install.is_native("macos") is False
    assert ENTRY.install.is_native("windows") is False
    assert isinstance(installer_for(ENTRY, platform_id=lambda: "linux"), Installer)
    assert isinstance(installer_for(ENTRY, platform_id=lambda: "macos"), Installer)
    assert isinstance(installer_for(ENTRY, platform_id=lambda: "windows"), Installer)


def test_script_platforms_defaults_to_platforms_so_old_entries_mean_what_they_said() -> None:
    assert TBC.install.script_platforms is None
    assert TBC.install.native is None
    assert TBC.install.scripted_platforms() == TBC.install.platforms
    assert TBC.install.uses_script("linux") is True
    assert TBC.install.is_native("linux") is False
    # And a platform the entry does not support is never "native" — that is the
    # honest 6.1 refusal, not an engine that starts and then fails.
    assert TBC.install.is_native("macos") is False
    assert TBC.install.is_native("windows") is False
    assert isinstance(installer_for(TBC, platform_id=lambda: "macos"), Installer)
    assert isinstance(installer_for(TBC, platform_id=lambda: "windows"), Installer)
```

and append to `tests/test_catalog.py`:

```python
def test_wotlk_names_no_script_platform_but_still_ships_its_scripts_until_7_2() -> None:
    """The flip's data half is one JSON key; the bash files and `script` stay until 7.2 (F.4 deletes this test)."""
    wotlk = load_catalog().get("wow-wotlk")
    assert wotlk.install.script_platforms is None
    assert wotlk.install.platforms == ("linux", "macos", "windows")
    assert wotlk.install.native is not None
```

```python
def test_ready_spec_defaults_and_the_azerothcore_builder() -> None:
    """`ReadySpec` is data; `azerothcore_ready()` is the legacy wrapper's two strings.

    The auth marker is `<host>:<port>` escaped, because `.` in a host is a regex
    wildcard and `10.0.0.1:8085` must not match `10x0y0z1:8085`.
    """
    spec = docker.ReadySpec(world="Avg Diff:")
    assert spec.auth is None and spec.fatal is None
    assert spec.timeout == docker._READY_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
    assert spec.interval == docker._POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS
    assert spec.restart_loop == 4
    ac = docker.azerothcore_ready("127.0.0.1", 8085, timeout=5.0, interval=0.1)
    assert ac.world == docker.AZEROTHCORE_READY_WORLD == "ready..."
    assert ac.auth == re.escape("127.0.0.1:8085")
    assert ac.timeout == 5.0 and ac.interval == 0.1 and ac.restart_loop == 4
    assert re.search(ac.auth, "Added realm at 127.0.0.1:8085")
    assert not re.search(ac.auth, "Added realm at 127x0x0x1:8085")
    with pytest.raises(TypeError, match="restart_loop"):
        docker.azerothcore_ready("127.0.0.1", 8085, restart_loop=2)


def test_container_state_reads_the_restart_count_in_the_same_inspect(
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
) -> None:
    """One `docker inspect` answers status, StartedAt AND RestartCount — never a second call."""
    seen: list[list[str]] = []

    def fake_run(
        cmd: list[str], cwd: Path | None = None, timeout: float | None = None
    ) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:
        seen.append(cmd)
        return _completed(stdout="restarting\t2026-08-22T01:24:53Z\t7\n")

    monkeypatch.setattr(docker.runner, "run", fake_run)
    state = docker.container_state("ac-worldserver")
    assert state == docker.ContainerState("restarting", "2026-08-22T01:24:53Z", 7)
    assert len(seen) == 1 and "{{.RestartCount}}" in seen[0][-1]
    # A two-field answer (older fakes, an unreadable count) still parses, count 0.
    monkeypatch.setattr(
        docker.runner, "run", lambda cmd, cwd=None, timeout=None: _completed(stdout="running\tT\n")
    )
    assert docker.container_state("x") == docker.ContainerState("running", "T", 0)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation** — in `yulon/docker.py`: add `import re` to the imports (directly after `import os`). Replace the `ContainerState` class and the `container_state()` function (grep for `class ContainerState` / `def container_state`) with:

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ContainerState:
    """A container's status, the start time of its current run, and its restart count."""

    status: str = ""
    started_at: str = ""
    restart_count: int = 0

    @property
    def settled(self) -> bool:
        """True if it is running right now, rather than between restarts.

        Every compose service here carries `restart: unless-stopped`, and a
        container in restart backoff still appears in a plain `docker ps` while
        `.State.StartedAt` holds the previous run's timestamp. A crash-looping
        worldserver therefore satisfies both of `wait_ready()`'s other checks
        and is reported ready — the same false pass the `--since` scoping was
        added to remove, arriving by a different route (review, 2026-08-22).
        """
        return self.status == "running"


def container_state(container: str) -> ContainerState:
    """Status, current-run start time and restart count in ONE `docker inspect`.

    One call, not two, because `wait_ready()` asks for all of it every two
    seconds for up to eight minutes and a `docker inspect` costs about 0.3s of
    CLI startup on its own — two of them per container per poll took a healthy
    poll from five docker invocations to seven, enough to overrun the interval
    it names (review, 2026-08-22). `RestartCount` rides along for the same
    price: it is how `wait_ready()` tells a crash loop from a slow start.
    """
    fmt = "{{.State.Status}}\t{{.State.StartedAt}}\t{{.RestartCount}}"
    proc = _docker(["inspect", container, "--format", fmt])
    if proc.returncode != 0:
        logger.warning(f"could not read the state of {container}: {proc.stderr.strip()}")
        return ContainerState()
    fields = [part.strip() for part in proc.stdout.strip().split("\t")]
    status, started, count = (fields + ["", "", ""])[:3]
    return ContainerState(status, started, int(count) if count.isdigit() else 0)
```

Then add, directly above `def wait_ready(` (grep for it):

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ReadySpec:
    """What "the server is up" means for one game, as data (phase7-decisions, Ready).

    Every string is a regex applied with `re.search` to the CURRENT run's log
    only. `auth=None` means the game has no separate auth container worth
    waiting on; `fatal` names a line that means the server will never become
    ready, so the wait ends at once instead of polling out its timeout;
    `restart_loop` is how much `RestartCount` may grow before a crash loop is
    declared. The AzerothCore values come from `azerothcore_ready()`, every
    other family's from `catalog.json`'s `ready` block (the spine `re.escape`s
    those unless the block says `regex: true` — contract amendment A5).
    """

    world: str
    auth: str | None = None
    fatal: str | None = None
    timeout: float = _READY_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
    interval: float = _POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS
    restart_loop: int = 4


AZEROTHCORE_READY_WORLD = "ready..."
"""What an AzerothCore worldserver prints once the world is loaded; the legacy marker."""


def azerothcore_ready(realm_host: str, realm_port: int, **kwargs: float) -> ReadySpec:
    """The AzerothCore `ReadySpec`: auth serving `<host>:<port>`, world saying `ready...`.

    The `**kwargs: float` shape is kept for `controller.wait_ready()` and
    `docker_ctl.wait_server_ready()`, whose callers forward `timeout`/`interval`
    exactly as they always did. Only those two are accepted: `restart_loop` is
    an int and anything else is a typo, so both are refused rather than dropped.
    The auth marker is escaped here, always: a host is a literal, not a regex.
    """
    unknown = set(kwargs) - {"timeout", "interval"}
    if unknown:
        raise TypeError(f"azerothcore_ready() accepts timeout/interval only, not {sorted(unknown)}")
    return ReadySpec(
        world=AZEROTHCORE_READY_WORLD,
        auth=re.escape(f"{realm_host}:{realm_port}"),
        timeout=kwargs.get("timeout", _READY_TIMEOUT_SECONDS),
        interval=kwargs.get("interval", _POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS),
    )
```

```python
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
    *,
    world_log: str,
    auth_log: str = "Added realm at 127.0.0.1:8085\n",
    inspect: Callable[[], str] | None = None,

        return _completed()

def test_wait_ready_declares_a_crash_loop_when_the_restart_count_keeps_growing(
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch, caplog: pytest.LogCaptureFixture
) -> None:
    """`RestartCount` growing by `restart_loop` since the first look is a loop, not a slow start.

    with caplog.at_level("WARNING", logger="yulon.docker"):

    polls = len([c for c in seen if c[:2] == ["docker", "ps"]])
    assert polls < 10, f"polled {polls} times; the loop was not detected"
    assert "restart" in caplog.text.lower()
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation** — replace `wait_ready()` (grep for `def wait_ready(` in yulon/docker.py) with:

```python
def wait_ready(auth_container: str, world_container: str, spec: ReadySpec) -> bool:
    """Poll until the server is up, or until it is clear it never will be.

    Ready means: every container the spec waits on is listed by `docker ps`
    AND running (not in restart backoff), and this run's log matches
    `spec.auth` (when set) / `spec.world`. Not-ready-ever means one of three
    things, each returning False at once: `spec.fatal` matched in the world
    log; `RestartCount` grew by `spec.restart_loop` since the first look (a
    crash loop under `restart: unless-stopped`, which `docker ps` never shows
    — review, 2026-08-22); or the timeout ran out.

    Logs are read into Python and matched here with `re.search`, never through
    a shell pipeline: the Tortoise script recorded `pipefail` + `grep -q`
    SIGPIPEing `docker logs` into false negatives. Both markers are looked for
    in the CURRENT run's logs only (see `_logs()`): Docker keeps output across
    restarts, and reading the whole log returned True on every restart while
    the server was in fact still loading.

    A transient `docker ps`/CLI failure is "not ready this iteration" and
    retried, never raised — see `_status_safe()`. A missing docker CLI is not
    transient: still `False`, said out loud at WARNING, and it stops within
    `_CLI_MISSING_GRACE_SECONDS` (fixed 2026-08-23).

    Worst-case wall-clock before `False` is `timeout + interval`, as for
    `wait_db_healthy()`. `spec.interval` must be positive (same reason).
    """
    if spec.interval <= 0:
        raise ValueError(f"interval must be positive, got {spec.interval!r}")
    logger.debug(
        f"wait_ready() called: auth_container={auth_container} "
        f"world_container={world_container} spec={spec}"
    )
    wanted = [world_container] if spec.auth is None else [auth_container, world_container]
    deadline = time.monotonic() + spec.timeout
    cli_missing_since: float | None = None
    first_restarts: int | None = None
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        try:
            running = _status_safe()
        except DockerCliMissingError:
            cli_missing_since, give_up = _cli_missing_run(cli_missing_since, "wait_ready()")
            if give_up:
                return False
            time.sleep(spec.interval)
            continue
        cli_missing_since = None
        if running is not None and all(name in running for name in wanted):
            # `docker ps` lists a container in restart backoff, so being listed
            # is not the same as being up. One inspect per container answers
            # that, "when did THIS run start" and "how often has it died".
            world = container_state(world_container)
            if first_restarts is None and world.status:
                first_restarts = world.restart_count
            if (
                first_restarts is not None
                and world.restart_count - first_restarts >= spec.restart_loop
            ):
                logger.warning(
                    f"{world_container} restarted {world.restart_count - first_restarts} times "
                    "while being waited on; that is a crash loop, not a slow start"
                )
                return False
            world_log = _logs(world_container, this_run_only=True, since=world.started_at)
            if spec.fatal is not None and re.search(spec.fatal, world_log):
                logger.warning(f"{world_container} printed a fatal line ({spec.fatal!r}); giving up")
                return False
            if (
                world.settled
                and re.search(spec.world, world_log)
                and _auth_ready(auth_container, spec)
            ):
                return True
        time.sleep(spec.interval)
    return False


def _auth_ready(auth_container: str, spec: ReadySpec) -> bool:
    """The auth half of `wait_ready()`: trivially true when the spec does not wait on auth."""
    if spec.auth is None:
        return True
    auth = container_state(auth_container)
    if not auth.settled:
        return False
    auth_log = _logs(auth_container, this_run_only=True, since=auth.started_at)
    return re.search(spec.auth, auth_log) is not None
```

```python
def wait_ready_for(spec: ContainerSpec, ready: ReadySpec) -> bool:
    """`wait_ready()` for `spec.auth`/`spec.world` with the game's `ReadySpec`."""
    return wait_ready(spec.auth, spec.world, ready)
```

```python
        """Poll until auth+world are up and ready. `kwargs` forward timeout/interval."""
```

```python
def _done(stdout: str = "", returncode: int = 0) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:
    return subprocess.CompletedProcess([], returncode, stdout, "")


def test_selinux_enforcing_reads_selinuxfs_first(
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch, tmp_path: Path
) -> None:
    """`/sys/fs/selinux/enforce` is the kernel's own answer; `getenforce` is the fallback."""
    monkeypatch.setattr(platform.sys, "platform", "linux")
    enforce = tmp_path / "enforce"
    enforce.write_text("1", encoding="utf-8")
    never = lambda argv: (_ for _ in ()).throw(AssertionError(argv))  # noqa: E731
    assert platform.selinux_enforcing(enforce_path=enforce, run=never, which=lambda n: None)
    enforce.write_text("0\n", encoding="utf-8")
    assert platform.selinux_enforcing(enforce_path=enforce, run=never, which=lambda n: None) is False


def test_selinux_enforcing_falls_back_to_getenforce_then_to_absent(
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch, tmp_path: Path
) -> None:
    monkeypatch.setattr(platform.sys, "platform", "linux")
    missing = tmp_path / "no-selinuxfs"
    calls: list[list[str]] = []

    def run(argv: list[str]) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:
        calls.append(argv)
        return _done("Enforcing\n")

    which = lambda name: "/usr/sbin/getenforce" if name == "getenforce" else None  # noqa: E731
    assert platform.selinux_enforcing(enforce_path=missing, run=run, which=which) is True
    assert calls == [["getenforce"]]
    assert platform.selinux_enforcing(
        enforce_path=missing, run=lambda a: _done("Permissive\n"), which=which
    ) is False
    # No selinuxfs and no getenforce: SELinux is not on this box — that is a "no", not a shrug.
    assert platform.selinux_enforcing(enforce_path=missing, run=run, which=lambda n: None) is False
    # getenforce present but broken: could not tell.
    assert platform.selinux_enforcing(
        enforce_path=missing, run=lambda a: _done("", 1), which=which
    ) is None


def test_selinux_enforcing_is_none_off_linux(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
    monkeypatch.setattr(platform.sys, "platform", "win32")
    assert platform.selinux_enforcing(run=lambda a: _done("1"), which=lambda n: "x") is None


def test_filesystem_type_asks_stat_for_the_first_existing_ancestor(
    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch, tmp_path: Path
) -> None:
    """The server dir usually does not exist yet at preflight; its parent's filesystem is the answer."""
    monkeypatch.setattr(platform.sys, "platform", "linux")
    calls: list[list[str]] = []

    def run(argv: list[str]) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:
        calls.append(argv)
        return _done("ext2/ext3\n")

    assert platform.filesystem_type(tmp_path / "wow" / "deeper", run=run) == "ext2/ext3"
    assert calls == [["stat", "-f", "-c", "%T", str(tmp_path)]]
    assert platform.filesystem_type(tmp_path, run=lambda a: _done("", 1)) is None
    monkeypatch.setattr(platform.sys, "platform", "darwin")
    assert platform.filesystem_type(tmp_path, run=run) is None


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("fs_type", "supported"),
    [
        (None, True),
        ("ext2/ext3", True),
        ("btrfs", True),
        ("xfs", True),
        ("ntfs", False),
        ("NTFS3", False),
        ("fuseblk", False),
        ("exfat", False),
        ("nfs4", False),
        ("9p", False),
    ],
)
def test_selinux_labels_supported_is_a_deny_list(fs_type: str | None, supported: bool) -> None:
    """Unknown means labels work (the common case); only the listed ones cannot carry them."""
    assert platform.selinux_labels_supported(fs_type) is supported


def test_bind_label_is_z_only_when_enforcing_on_a_labelable_filesystem() -> None:
    assert platform.bind_label(enforcing=True, fs_type="ext2/ext3") == ":z"
    assert platform.bind_label(enforcing=True, fs_type=None) == ":z"
    assert platform.bind_label(enforcing=True, fs_type="ntfs") == ""
    assert platform.bind_label(enforcing=False, fs_type="ext2/ext3") == ""
    assert platform.bind_label(enforcing=None, fs_type="ext2/ext3") == ""
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation** — insert in `yulon/platform.py` directly after `docker_engine_commands()` returns (grep for `def docker_engine_commands`; the insertion point is the blank lines before the `# ------ downloads` comment block):

```python
# ---------------------------------------------------------------- SELinux
# Fedora and its family run SELinux enforcing, and a bind mount without a
# label is unreadable to the container. The WotLK bash installer's answer —
# `:z` on every host bind, and `chcon -Rt container_file_t` on the bind roots,
# skipped on filesystems that cannot carry a label — passed the 2026-08-25
# Fedora gate. These are the same facts, as functions, so composegen's
# `BIND_LABEL` token and preflight's SELinux line read one source.

SELINUX_NOLABEL_FS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    {"exfat", "ntfs", "ntfs3", "fuseblk", "msdos", "vfat", "cifs", "smb2", "nfs", "nfs4", "9p"}
)
"""Filesystems that cannot hold an SELinux label; `:z` on them makes the daemon refuse the mount."""

_SELINUX_ENFORCE_PATH = Path("/sys/fs/selinux/enforce")


def selinux_enforcing(
    *,
    enforce_path: Path = _SELINUX_ENFORCE_PATH,
    run: RunCmd | None = None,
    which: Callable[[str], str | None] | None = None,
) -> bool | None:
    """Is SELinux enforcing here? `None` = could not tell (or not Linux at all).

    The kernel's own file first, then `getenforce`. A box with neither has no
    SELinux, and that is a definite "not enforcing", not an unknown — Ubuntu
    and Arch must not get an "unchecked" line for a subsystem they lack.
    """
    if detect() != "linux":
        return None
    try:
        if enforce_path.exists():
            return enforce_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip() == "1"
    except OSError as exc:
        logger.info(f"could not read {enforce_path}: {exc}")
    find = which if which is not None else _which
    if find("getenforce") is None:
        return False
    do = run if run is not None else (lambda argv: runner.run(argv, timeout=5.0))
    try:
        proc = do(["getenforce"])
    except OSError as exc:
        logger.info(f"getenforce could not be started: {exc}")
        return None
    if proc.returncode != 0:
        return None
    return proc.stdout.strip().lower() == "enforcing"


def filesystem_type(path: Path, *, run: RunCmd | None = None) -> str | None:
    """`stat -f -c %T` of the first existing ancestor of `path`; Linux only, `None` if unknown.

    The ancestor walk is `preflight._free_bytes()`'s, for the same reason: the
    server folder is routinely one the user has not created yet.
    """
    if detect() != "linux":
        return None
    probe = path
    while not probe.exists() and probe != probe.parent:
        probe = probe.parent
    do = run if run is not None else (lambda argv: runner.run(argv, timeout=5.0))
    try:
        proc = do(["stat", "-f", "-c", "%T", str(probe)])
    except OSError as exc:
        logger.info(f"stat could not be started: {exc}")
        return None
    if proc.returncode != 0 or not proc.stdout.strip():
        return None
    return proc.stdout.strip().lower()


def selinux_labels_supported(fs_type: str | None) -> bool:
    """Deny-list: an unknown filesystem is assumed to hold labels, because most do."""
    return fs_type is None or fs_type.lower() not in SELINUX_NOLABEL_FS


def bind_label(*, enforcing: bool | None, fs_type: str | None) -> str:
    """The compose bind-mount suffix: `:z` under enforcing SELinux on a labelable filesystem, else ``.

    Empty off SELinux so every template renders byte-identical there, which
    `test_composegen.py`'s byte assertions and the compose-config fixture prove.
    """
    return ":z" if enforcing is True and selinux_labels_supported(fs_type) else ""
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation** — append to the SELinux section in `yulon/platform.py` (after `bind_label()`):

```python
def relabel_for_containers(
    path: Path,
    *,
    run: RunCmd | None = None,
    which: Callable[[str], str | None] | None = None,
) -> bool:
    """`chcon -Rt container_file_t <path>` — label our own files so a container may read them.

    No sudo: the server dir is the user's, so the user may relabel it. A
    failure is a warning and `False`, never an error — the `:z` on the bind
    lines is the mechanism that carries the install; this is belt and braces
    for the files that already exist before compose runs.
    """
    find = which if which is not None else _which
    if find("chcon") is None:
        logger.warning(f"could not relabel {path}: chcon is not installed")
        return False
    do = run if run is not None else (lambda argv: runner.run(argv, timeout=600.0))
    try:
        proc = do(["chcon", "-Rt", "container_file_t", str(path)])
    except OSError as exc:
        logger.warning(f"could not relabel {path}: {exc}")
        return False
    if proc.returncode != 0:
        logger.warning(f"could not relabel {path}: chcon exit {proc.returncode} {proc.stderr.strip()}")
        return False
    return True


def container_user_args(*, platform_id: Callable[[], PlatformId] = detect) -> list[str]:
    """`["--user", "uid:gid"]` for `docker run` on Linux; `[]` on Docker Desktop.

    The one home for this policy (phase7-decisions, Extraction): on Linux a
    bind mount written by the image's root is owned by root on the host and
    the user cannot delete their own install; Docker Desktop maps ownership to
    the logged-in user, so there the image's user is right and `--user` would
    only break images that expect to be root. `git.ContainerGit` and 7.3's
    `docker.run_container()` both ask here rather than deciding for themselves.
    """
    if platform_id() != "linux":
        return []
    getuid = getattr(os, "getuid", None)
    getgid = getattr(os, "getgid", None)
    if getuid is None or getgid is None:
        return []
    return ["--user", f"{getuid()}:{getgid()}"]
```

Replace `ContainerGit._user_args` in `yulon/git.py` (grep for `def _user_args`) with:

```python
    @staticmethod
    def _user_args() -> list[str]:
        """`platform.container_user_args()` — the policy lives there, not here."""
        return platform.container_user_args()
```

(`os` stays imported in `git.py`: `_no_prompt_env()` still uses `os.environ` — grep for it before removing any import.)

```python
INHIBIT_ARGV = [
    "systemd-inhibit",
    "--what=idle:sleep",
    "--who=Yu'lon",
    "--why=installing a server",
    "sleep",
    "infinity",
]


def test_keep_awake_on_linux_holds_a_systemd_inhibit_and_lets_it_go() -> None:
    """The `caffeinate` shape on Linux: a detached inhibitor, terminated on the way out."""
    spawned: list[list[str]] = []
    stopped: list[str] = []

    class FakeChild:
        def terminate(self) -> None:
            stopped.append("terminated")

    with platform.keep_awake(
        platform_id=lambda: "linux",
        spawn=lambda argv: (spawned.append(argv), FakeChild())[1],  # type: ignore[arg-type,return-value]
    ):
        assert spawned == [INHIBIT_ARGV]
        assert stopped == []
    assert stopped == ["terminated"]


def test_keep_awake_on_linux_survives_a_missing_systemd_inhibit(
    caplog: pytest.LogCaptureFixture,
) -> None:
    """No systemd (or no binary) is a warning, and the install still runs."""

    def refuse(_argv: list[str]) -> object:
        raise OSError("no such file")

    with caplog.at_level("WARNING"):
        with platform.keep_awake(platform_id=lambda: "linux", spawn=refuse):  # type: ignore[arg-type]
            pass
    assert "may be interrupted" in caplog.text
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation** — in `keep_awake()` (grep for `def keep_awake(`): replace the last paragraph of the docstring (from `Linux: a no-op for Phase 6.` through `written blind here.`) with:

```
    Linux: `systemd-inhibit --what=idle:sleep ... sleep infinity`, detached,
    terminated on exit — the `caffeinate` shape. Unlike `caffeinate -w` it
    does not die with us on its own, so the `finally` matters here. A box
    without systemd gets the same warning as a Mac without `caffeinate`.
```

and replace the body from `here = platform_id()` through the macOS branch's `return` with a shared helper (the macOS branch becomes one call; the Windows branch and the trailing `logger.debug(...)` + `yield` no-op are unchanged):

```python
    here = platform_id()
    if here == "macos":
        with _held_by(["caffeinate", "-dims", "-w", str(os.getpid())], "this Mac", spawn):
            yield
        return
    if here == "linux":
        argv = [
            "systemd-inhibit",
            "--what=idle:sleep",
            "--who=Yu'lon",
            "--why=installing a server",
            "sleep",
            "infinity",
        ]
        with _held_by(argv, "this machine", spawn):
            yield
        return
```

and add, directly above `def _spawn_detached(`:

```python
@contextmanager
def _held_by(
    argv: list[str],
    what: str,
    spawn: Callable[[list[str]], subprocess.Popen[bytes]] | None,
) -> Iterator[None]:
    """Spawn a keep-awake helper for the block; a helper that will not start is a warning."""
    start = spawn if spawn is not None else _spawn_detached
    try:
        child = start(argv)
    except OSError as exc:
        logger.warning(f"could not hold {what} awake ({exc}); the build may be interrupted")
        yield
        return
    logger.info(f"holding {what} awake for the build: {' '.join(argv)}")
    try:
        yield
    finally:
        child.terminate()
```

### Task C.6: docker-buildx on the dnf and pacman engine lists

**Interfaces:**
